|<p>**I. Software Test Plan Document Purpose:** </p><p>The purpose of this document is to ensure that the software developed will meet client satisfaction and expectations  as  per  requirements  defined  clearly.  To  validate  that  the  system  has  robustness, efficiency, and adaptability, this document intends to show that all the requirements of the clients are </p>|
| - |
|duly met. Besides, it ensures that the plan is practical enough to meet the standards and regulations to |
|comply with. At the final level, the Software Testing Plan (STP) would also act as a framework and |
|planning guide in executing and applying the tests. |
|**II. Software Test Plan Contents:** |
||
|A test plan provides testing execution strategy during the testing session. It gives an overall view of almost all the aspects, like objectives, scope, risks, strategy, approach/technique, participating teams, test objects, resources, entry and exit criteria, test prioritization, and tool support. Moreover, it also describes the modules as well as their functionalities. |
|**III. Software Test Plan Document in term of topics to be covered:** |
|This document intends to bring together several vital elements, including the testing strategy, scope, |
|risks and contingencies, entry and exit criteria, and item pass or fail criteria, among others. Further, it |
|will offer a description of the Test Specification, which would simplify and portray the existing test cases |
|being developed. The Test Environment will show all the necessary facilities, hardware, the software |
|necessary, and other requirements to ensure seamless execution during the tests. Finally, this will include the Test Schedule, Staff Responsibilities, and the Training needs focusing on the roles of those personnel in the overall schedule. |
|All items in this document are designed to align with and address the planning and design details |
|outlined in the Software Requirement Specification (SRS), ensuring the project meets its defined |
|objectives. |

1. **TEST<a name="_page9_x69.00_y130.92"></a> POLICY** 

This  section  explains  major  philosophy  of  the  organization  of  Vitamin  Lab  (dummy organization) regarding testing and its terms, meaning and practices. However, most of these policy aspects are widely applied in any other organization even UniKL MIIT may have this in their test policy since this is based on ISTQB (worldwide standard). 

1. **Software<a name="_page9_x69.00_y248.92"></a> Testing Term Definition** 

Testing is a development activity that is all about process of finding and identifying defects by performing test and checking the actual result match the expected results or not. It also known as process of verification and validation which is based on ISO-9000 standard as shown below:  

- **Validation** - Confirmation by examination and through the provision of objective evidence  that  specified  requirements  have  been  fulfilled.  To  summarize  ISO statement, validation confirms that product build with correct requirement that satisfy the customer requirement. 
- **Verification** - Confirmation by examination and through provision of objective evidence that the requirements for a specific intended use or application have been fulfilled. To summarize the ISO statement, verification confirms that software has been created and fulfilled. 

Testing also defines what will happen once defect occur (failure). Testing is performed by testing team which then the report/result will be forwarded to developer team so that they can start debugging. 

<table><tr><th colspan="1" rowspan="3" valign="top">![](eb73119a-d7c3-4901-8974-9decbd7603e1_00000.005.png)</th><th colspan="1" valign="top">ORGANISATION: </th><th colspan="1">PERFECT WAVE ACADEMY </th><th colspan="1" valign="top">SECTION: </th><th colspan="1" valign="top">SE </th></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">AREA BASED ON: </td><td colspan="1">SETIAWANGSA, KUALA LUMPUR </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">PAGE: </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">10/256            </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">DOCUMENT TITLE: </td><td colspan="3"><b>ACCEPTANCE TEST PLAN</b> FOR  Perfect Wave Academy (PWA): Swimming Class Management System </td></tr>
</table>

2. **Advantages<a name="_page10_x69.00_y130.92"></a> of Testing** 

There are several significant advantages of testing that can produce magnificent software application with supreme quality. These are numerous advantage that can be extract from testing: 

- Testing **increases quality** in terms of the software attribute indirectly in which defect can be detected and corrected earlier prior to delivery. In addition to that, testing did **increase process quality** as well since defects are documented which means it can be analyzed, processed and solution to that error can be reused again to prevent same error again. 
- Testing **boosts confidence** of all stakeholders which affects perception of team management regarding the software quality as well its robustness due to very few or no failure are found. 
- Testing is like **liability insurance** where it costs big money, but it ensures all the stakeholders to be at ease as it cover and mitigate all the possible risks. 
- Testing that is successfully conducted leads to **major reductions in cost** as defects can cause huge costs. 

<table><tr><th colspan="1" rowspan="3" valign="top">![](eb73119a-d7c3-4901-8974-9decbd7603e1_00000.005.png)</th><th colspan="1" valign="top">ORGANISATION: </th><th colspan="1">PERFECT WAVE ACADEMY </th><th colspan="1" valign="top">SECTION: </th><th colspan="1" valign="top">SE </th></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">AREA BASED ON: </td><td colspan="1">SETIAWANGSA, KUALA LUMPUR </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">PAGE: </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">11/256            </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">DOCUMENT TITLE: </td><td colspan="3"><b>ACCEPTANCE TEST PLAN</b> FOR  Perfect Wave Academy (PWA): Swimming Class Management System </td></tr>
</table>

<a name="_page11_x69.00_y130.92"></a>**1.3. Testing Principles** 

There are seven testing principles that this organization applies which are based on ISTQB standard. 

**Principle 1: Testing shows presence of defects.** 

Testing can determine and reveal defects are present but unable to verify and identify that there are no defects at all are  presents.  According  to  Edger  W.  Dijkstra,  1970 “Program testing can be  used to show presence of bugs, but never to show their absence!” 

**Principle 2: Exhaustive testing is impossible.** 

Full complete/exhaustive testing is truly not feasible except for trivial  situations.  Risk-based  testing  will  be  used  to produce  risk  analysis  to  determine  priorities  of  features module that need to be carried out. 

**Principle 3: Early testing** 

Testing is not a phase that happens at the end of software development,  it  should  start  as  early  as  possible.  Early testing (like static testing; review) takes place parallel to development activities,  defects  are  detected  earlier  thus cost less to fix. 

**Principle 4: Defect clustering** 

Testing  effort  shall  be  focused  proportionally  to  the expected and observed defect density of modules. A small number  of  modules  often  contain  most  of  the  defects discovered  during  pre-release  testing/or  responsible  for most of operational failures. 

<table><tr><th colspan="1" rowspan="3" valign="top">![](eb73119a-d7c3-4901-8974-9decbd7603e1_00000.005.png)</th><th colspan="1" valign="top">ORGANISATION: </th><th colspan="1">PERFECT WAVE ACADEMY </th><th colspan="1" valign="top">SECTION: </th><th colspan="1" valign="top">SE </th></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">AREA BASED ON: </td><td colspan="1">SETIAWANGSA, KUALA LUMPUR </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">PAGE: </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">12/256            </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">DOCUMENT TITLE: </td><td colspan="3"><b>ACCEPTANCE TEST PLAN</b> FOR  Perfect Wave Academy (PWA): Swimming Class Management System </td></tr>
</table>

**Principle 5: Repetitions have no effects – Pesticide Paradox** Repeating test brings no new insight thus test cases  need  to  be  regularly  reviewed,  revised,  and modified. So that we won’t execute the same test and test cases repeatedly, there is a need for changes. 

**Principle 6: Testing is context dependent.** 

Testing depends on the context usage of that software. For example,  safety-critical  software  systems  are  TESTED DIFFERENTLY  (more  intensive  in  technique)  from commercial applications. 

**Principle 7: Absence of errors fallacy** 

System without failures does not imply system will meet user  expectations.  Sometimes  there  are  no  errors  and failures, but the functionalities given are not what the user wants and expected. 

4. **Test<a name="_page12_x69.00_y543.92"></a> Missions & Objectives** 

Delivering the software product at its finest quality is this organization's goal where the functional and quality attributes are satisfactory, defect presence is almost minimal, testing activities are conducted systematically. 

<table><tr><th colspan="1" rowspan="3" valign="top">![](eb73119a-d7c3-4901-8974-9decbd7603e1_00000.005.png)</th><th colspan="1" valign="top">ORGANISATION: </th><th colspan="1">PERFECT WAVE ACADEMY </th><th colspan="1" valign="top">SECTION: </th><th colspan="1" valign="top">SE </th></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">AREA BASED ON: </td><td colspan="1">SETIAWANGSA, KUALA LUMPUR </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">PAGE: </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">13/256            </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">DOCUMENT TITLE: </td><td colspan="3"><b>ACCEPTANCE TEST PLAN</b> FOR  Perfect Wave Academy (PWA): Swimming Class Management System </td></tr>
</table>

5. **Communication<a name="_page13_x69.00_y130.92"></a> Among Stakeholders** 

It is important for all related stakeholders to participate in the testing process and give insight/feedback regarding the software product. Open minded, professional, analytical and straight forward attribute is a must for the testing to perform smoothly according to what being planned. 

6. **SDLC<a name="_page13_x69.00_y229.92"></a> Identification** 

The  Rapid  Application  Development  (RAD)  model,  as  the  methodology  in  the  Systems Development Life Cycle (SDLC), is adopted by Perfect Wave Academy (PWA) in developing Swimming Class Management System. 

Rapid Application Development chosen mainly because of the nature of the project in terms of quick prototyping and making it iterative through all features and modules like Student Module, Coach Module, Notification Module, payment plan, self-learning module. RAD is able to change its demands as new requirements emerge so that a project may progress even when all major details are not entirely known at the beginning. 

RAD is supposed to be faster in development and will give early feedback from stakeholders. The only thing is that this approach fits perfectly into the schemes of Perfect Wave Academy because the system contains modules that have a lot of user interaction such as student session booking and shopping. This is achieved through early involvement of the users in the testing of the modules. 

In addition, RAD's empirical approach to development and testing offers early integration of activities  like  preparation,  planning,  execution,  and  so  on  in  testing  into  the  very  first development stages. Rapid prototyping and allowing user evaluation during each iteration will increase  opportunities  for  developer-stakeholder  collaboration  with  relevant  insights  for system improvement. 

<table><tr><th colspan="1" rowspan="3" valign="top">![](eb73119a-d7c3-4901-8974-9decbd7603e1_00000.005.png)</th><th colspan="1" valign="top">ORGANISATION: </th><th colspan="1">PERFECT WAVE ACADEMY </th><th colspan="1" valign="top">SECTION: </th><th colspan="1" valign="top">SE </th></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">AREA BASED ON: </td><td colspan="1">SETIAWANGSA, KUALA LUMPUR </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">PAGE: </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">14/256            </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">DOCUMENT TITLE: </td><td colspan="3"><b>ACCEPTANCE TEST PLAN</b> FOR  Perfect Wave Academy (PWA): Swimming Class Management System </td></tr>
</table>

Through the RAD, the Self-Learning Module or Payment Plan Module functionality may be prototyped, tested, and improved iteratively to identify possible risks, bugs, and usability issues that may arise. This minimizes the likelihood of mistakes and permits the final system to be as robust and closely aligned to what the end-users really need. 

Last but not least, the reason why RAD is great for Swimming Class Management System is because of its high interactivity, encourages end-user feedback, and provides for constant change, which means delivery of the whole user-friendly system will be timely.

<a name="_page15_x69.00_y372.92"></a>*Figure 1 Rapid Application Development (RAD) Model Diagram* 



|**Phases** |**Description** |
| - | - |
|Requirement Planning |This phase focuses on gathering the high-level requirements of the project. Research is conducted to understand the system’s objectives, scope, and resources required for development. For your **Swimming Class Management System**, this is where you identify the key system features such as student booking, payment plans, coach schedules, and reporting. In the context of testing, this phase also includes the creation of a test plan at a high level, outlining the strategies and scope of testing to ensure all functional requirements will be validated. |
|User  Design (Prototype,  Test, Refine) |<p>This phase is focused on creating a **prototype** of the system that users can interact with. For the **Swimming Class Management System**, prototypes for modules like **Student Session Booking** or **Payment Plan  Module**  would  be  developed  rapidly.  The  prototype  helps visualize how the final system will function and allows for early user feedback.  </p><p>- **Prototype**: A basic version of the system is created, showcasing core functionalities like session booking and payment tracking.  </p>|

||<p>- **Test**: The prototype undergoes **User Acceptance Testing (UAT)**, where the client (e.g., students or coaches) evaluates the functionality and provides feedback. **Functional testing** is also done to ensure the system  works  as  per  the  **Software  Requirements  Specification (SRS)**. </p><p>- **Refine**: Based on feedback from testing, the prototype is refined. Any issues found during UAT or functional testing are addressed, and the system is improved. The **looping process** continues as the prototype is  iteratively  updated  based  on  client feedback  until  it meets  their expectations. </p>|
| :- | - |
|Construction |<p>Once the **prototype** is **validated** and **refined**, the system goes into **full development**. In this phase, all the **modules** of the system—say, **Coach Scheduling**, **Notification Module**, **Payment Plan**—are **coded** and  **integrated**  to  have  the  whole  system  **up  and  running**.  The **developers**  ensure  that  all  the  **features**  and  **requirements**  are **implemented** as **designed initially**, while **internal testing** activities such as **unit testing** and **integration testing** are carried out to identify and fix **technical issues**. </p><p>In this stage, the **prototype** is **tested extensively** using two most important  techniques:  **Functional  Testing**  and  **User  Acceptance Testing (UAT)**. </p><p>- **Functional Testing**: This testing is carried out to find **bugs**, **errors**, and **defects** in a system; that all **functionalities** are working properly according to the  **Software Test  Plan** and **Software  Requirements  Specification**.  Both  **documents** serve as a **reference** for the execution of tests. </p><p>- **UAT**: The system is exposed to the **client** in order to take note of their **general opinion** and **comments**. In case it doesn't **satisfy** the customer's **requirements**, the details will be **noted** </p>|

||<p>**down**, **analyzed**, and the **prototype updated** with the findings . </p><p>This may cause a new cycle of **analysis**, **design**, **implementation**, and  **testing**  as  the  system  is  **developed**  based  on  the **recommendations** given by the **client** or due to **new requirements**. The **cycles continue** until the **client** is completely **satisfied** with the **system**.  Since  this  is  **evolutionary  prototyping**,  the  **improved prototype** is not **thrown away**; it is incrementally **developed** into the **final working system**. The **deliverables** from this phase include a **stable**,  **functioning  system**  with  **frequent  evaluation**  and  **client feedback** to ensure the **final product** is as expected and **ready for deployment**. </p>|
| :- | :- |
|Cutover |The final phase involves the **deployment** of the system into production. In the case of the **Swimming Class Management System**, this means rolling out the final working version of the system to all users, such as students,  coaches,  and  administrators.  During  this  phase,  further **testing**  may  be  performed  to  ensure  everything  works  in  a  live environment. After deployment, **maintenance** routines are set up to monitor system performance, address issues, and provide ongoing support. The system is now in active use, and the feedback gathered during this phase could lead to future updates or improvements. |

2. **INTRODUCTION<a name="_page18_x69.00_y130.92"></a> TO TEST PLAN** 

The document outlines an Acceptance Test Plan to test the developed Dishcovery mobile application. ATP will be used by the acceptance test team to plan, conduct and document the acceptance testing. In this context of acceptance level, black box testing is used where it is specification-based testing technique that refer SRS to conduct a functional test where the test case is derived through use case testing which is based on use case specification from SRS. Hence during the testing period, several references of documentation are used which is stated at the references part. 

1. **System<a name="_page18_x69.00_y324.92"></a> Description** 

Perfect Wave Academy is a versatile swimming management platform designed to meet  the  needs  of  students,  coaches,  and  administrators.  This  innovative  web application simplifies the process of scheduling, booking, and managing swimming sessions, ensuring a smooth and user-friendly experience for everyone involved. With features  like  session  scheduling,  self-learning  resources,  and  secure  payment processing, the platform is tailored to support users in reaching their swimming goals. Perfect Wave Academy aims to create an engaging and efficient environment that enhances the learning experience while streamlining administrative tasks. 

**Modules lists for student:** 

- **Register Account :** 

Students can create an account by providing necessary details like name, email, and password to access the system features. 

- **Login / Logout:** 

Enables students to log in securely to their accounts and log out when their session is complete. 

- **QR Attendance Scan:** 

Students can scan QR codes at sessions to mark their attendance automatically. 

- **Attendance History:** 

Displays  a  record  of  all  past  sessions  attended  by the  student for

4. **Document<a name="_page25_x69.00_y130.92"></a> Overview** 

The STP document consists of six sections as brief below:  

- **Section 1** contains **Test Policy** components which explain the major philosophy of the development organization regarding their testing and its terms, testing SOP (Standard of Procedure), meaning and practices.  
- **Section 2** explains **Introduction to Test Plan** which gives an overview about the current acceptance test plan purpose, system description, references, and document structure overview.  
- **Section 3** contains **Test Strategy** which describes the testing approach, test objective, test scope, test process, test level, risk and contingency, entry and exit criteria, item pass/fail criteria, suspension criteria and resumption requirements.  
- **Section 4** contains **Test Case Specification** which describes the current test cases in development along with Use Case Diagram.  
- **Section 5** contains **Test Environment** which describes all the required facilities, hardware, software, and other necessary requirements for test to be executed smoothly.  
- **Section 6** contains **Test Schedule**, which discusses an overall schedule plan including testing activities.

3. **TEST<a name="_page26_x69.00_y130.92"></a> STRATEGY** 

A test strategy is an outline that describes the testing approach of the software development cycle where it describes how the testing activity will be done. It is the main core part of this test plan. 

1. **Test<a name="_page26_x69.00_y242.92"></a> Objective** 

The main objective of this test plan for the **Swimming Class Management System** is to ensure the application runs smoothly, is reliable, and delivers the intended features without  issues.  The  testing  process  will  focus on  finding  and  fixing  any  bugs  or problems to make sure the system works properly for all users, including students, coaches, admins, and guests. The testing will also ensure that the system meets the goals  outlined  in  the  Software  Requirements  Specification  (SRS)  and  satisfies stakeholders' expectations. 

Here are the main objectives: 

1. **Check the Requirements** 

Test the system to confirm it matches all the requirements listed in the SRS document. 

2. **Test All Features** 

Make sure every feature works, including Login/Logout, QR Attendance Scan, Session Management, Timetable Viewing, Notifications, Tutorial Videos, the Forum, Shopping, Cashless Payments, Progress Tracking, and Reports/Statistics. 

3. **User-Friendly Design** 

Ensure the system is easy to use, visually appealing, and provides a smooth experience for all users. 

4. **Get Approval** 

Gather feedback and approval from stakeholders (e.g., your project supervisor and client) to confirm the system meets their needs. 

5. **Follow Testing Guidelines** 

Use standardized testing methods to ensure the process is organized and effective. 

<table><tr><th colspan="1" rowspan="3" valign="top">![](eb73119a-d7c3-4901-8974-9decbd7603e1_00000.005.png)</th><th colspan="1" valign="top">ORGANISATION: </th><th colspan="1">PERFECT WAVE ACADEMY </th><th colspan="1" valign="top">SECTION: </th><th colspan="1" valign="top">SE </th></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">AREA BASED ON: </td><td colspan="1">SETIAWANGSA, KUALA LUMPUR </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">PAGE: </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">27/256            </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">DOCUMENT TITLE: </td><td colspan="3"><b>ACCEPTANCE TEST PLAN</b> FOR  Perfect Wave Academy (PWA): Swimming Class Management System </td></tr>
</table>

6. **Test Performance** 

Check how well the system handles different situations, ensuring it responds quickly and uses resources efficiently. 

7. **Protect User Data** 

Perform security checks to ensure sensitive information, like user data and payments, is safe. 

8. **Make it Accessible** 

Verify that the system can be used by people with various abilities, following accessibility guidelines. 

9. **Prevent Issues After Updates** 

Test the system after updates to ensure new changes don’t break existing features. 

10. **Prepare for Real-World Use** 

Ensure the system is ready for public use after passing all necessary tests. 

11. **Improve Based on Feedback** 

Listen to feedback from stakeholders and users, making changes as needed to improve the overall experience. 

12. **Satisfy Stakeholders** 

Ensure the system meets expectations and is ready for launch, giving users confidence in its reliability and quality.

The item to be tested is Perfect Wave Academy Application Prototype. 

<a name="_page28_x69.00_y355.92"></a>**3.3.1. Features Tested**

4. **Test<a name="_page29_x69.00_y130.92"></a> Process**

Initially, there are five main processes involved in this approach. 

1. **Planning and Control** 
- **Planning**: 
- Define test objectives, test scope, and testing strategy to align with project requirements. 
- Review documents like the Software Requirement Specification (SRS) to identify testable requirements and potential test cases. 
- Develop a comprehensive test plan document detailing: 
  - Test objectives 
  - Test scope 
  - Test strategy and schedule 
  - Risks and mitigation plans 
  - Resources required (personnel, tools, hardware, software) 
- Construct test cases based on use cases identified in the SRS. 
- **Control**: 
- Monitor progress, ensuring the planned testing activities align with the schedule. 
- Adjust test plans dynamically based on project updates or identified risks. 
- Ensure quality and efficiency by reviewing and refining test cases and procedures.

2. **Analysis and Design** 
- **Analysis**: 
  - Evaluate the system and its requirements for testability. 
  - Review  the  identified  test  cases  and  ensure  they  comprehensively  cover functional and non-functional requirements. 
- **Design**: 
- Prepare the testing environment, ensuring tools, software, and hardware are available and functional. 
- Arrange entry criteria and ensure all dependencies (e.g., modules, data) are prepared. 
- Finalise test cases, scripts, and test data, ensuring clarity and traceability to the requirements. 
3. **Implementation and Execution** 
- **Implementation:** 
- Develop detailed test scripts or procedures based on the finalised test cases. 
- Integrate test scripts with tools and infrastructure for seamless execution. 
- Verify the readiness of the test environment. 
- **Execution:** 
- Run the tests according to the defined plan and scripts. 
- Compare actual results with expected outcomes to determine pass or fail status. 
- Record  actions  taken,  inputs,  outputs,  and  any  incidents  encountered  for documentation purposes.

4. **Evaluation and Report** 
- **Evaluation**: 
  - Analyse all identified incidents and defects, prioritising them based on severity and impact. 
  - Reassess test results against expectations to identify gaps or issues. 
- **Report**: 
- Share incident and defect reports with relevant stakeholders. 
- Ensure issues are addressed, retesting where necessary to verify fixes. 
- Document all results, lessons learned, and any challenges faced. 
5. **Test Closure** 
- Evaluate the exit criteria to determine if testing objectives have been met. 
- Ensure and check deliverables delivered or not  
- Closing  all  incident  reports,  ensuring  proper  documentation  for  future reference. 
- Archive testware, environment and infrastructure for next reuse usage  
- Evaluate how testing went and learn lessons for future reference 
5. **Item<a name="_page32_x69.00_y532.92"></a> Pass/Fail Criteria** 

The test oracle to determine pass and fail in test level is the functional  

requirements or use case that are based on Perfect Wave Academy SRS that consists of requirements and use case specification.  

If the test fails to meet the objectives of any of the functional  requirements or use cases, then it is considered as a failure thus a defect or  incident will be documented and reported where it will be reviewed by the  tester and developer.

6. **Suspension<a name="_page33_x69.00_y130.92"></a> Criteria and Resumption Requirements** 
1. **Suspension<a name="_page33_x69.00_y172.92"></a> Criteria**  

Testing will only stop if the mobile app suddenly becomes unavailable or there are physical disturbance and disaster in test environment like core resources (electric) and natural disaster. 

2. **Suspension<a name="_page33_x69.00_y292.92"></a> Criteria**  

If testing did stop where it in the case of mobile app unavailability, testing will be resumed once it becomes available again. In addition to that, for physical disturbance and disaster, it can resume once the test environment becomes stable again.

7. **Entry<a name="_page34_x69.00_y130.92"></a> and Exit Criteria** 

This section will specify when to start the testing activities and when to stop the testing activities. This is essential and required to be mentioned in the test plan as exhaustive testing is impossible. 

1. **Entrance<a name="_page34_x69.00_y248.92"></a> Criteria** 

Below are the criteria required to start the testing activities (Execution) 

- All required tools, whether in the form of hardware or software for the test environment, are available. 
- The  test  environment  has  been  set  up  and  prepared  with  reliable  quality beforehand. 
- Requirements are documented in SRS, reviewed, and approved by relevant stakeholders (supervisor specifically). 
- Previous tests in system, integration and unit test level have been conducted and satisfactory.

2. **Exit<a name="_page35_x69.00_y130.92"></a> Criteria** 

Below are the criteria required to stop the testing activities. 

- All test cases specified executed successfully based on test coverage of 80%.  Test coverage which defines how much testing conducted should achieve above 80%. This can be achieved by calculating the formula below. 

**for example,**  

C: total test cases executed / total number of test cases \* 100%  C: 35/43 \* 100% = 81.39% 

- All listed high priority modules with defects are fixed. 
- All unresolved defects are documented in test summary report with details such as priority and severity. 
- Software development completed within planned timelines.

9. **Test<a name="_page38_x69.00_y130.92"></a> Level** 

The Swimming Class Management System (Perfect Wave Academy) incorporates various testing levels to ensure that each functionality meets its intended purpose. The primary test level to be conducted for this project is Acceptance Testing. 

**Acceptance Testing** 

At this level, the system is evaluated to confirm whether it meets all specified requirements and operates as intended for end-users. This testing phase ensures the application is ready for real-world deployment. To achieve this, a black-box testing approach is utilized, focusing solely on system functionality without examining the internal code. The testing will rely on the Software  Requirements  Specification  (SRS)  document  as  its  foundation.  It  will  involve students, coaches, and admins, with the assistance of developers, to validate the viability of the system at the testing site. 

1. **Black<a name="_page38_x69.00_y386.92"></a> Box Testing** 

The black-box testing approach focuses on validating the system's input and output. 

This method is particularly suitable as it only assesses functionalities without delving into the underlying code. For this project, it is referred to as Specification-Based Testing because it strictly adheres to the SRS document. 

1. ***Functional Testing (Test Type)*** 

Functional testing is performed to confirm that every feature of the system works as 

intended. Each functionality is tested against its defined behavior in the SRS. This ensures  that  modules  like  QR  Attendance  Scan,  Notifications,  and  Session Management perform correctly and consistently. 

2. ***Use Case Based Testing*** 

Use Case-Based Testing is a method for determining if the workflows described in the 

system's use cases perform properly in real-world scenarios. Each use case describes a specific interaction between the user and the system to achieve a specific objective, making this method excellent for testing functionality from the user's perspective. This approach  transforms  the  use  cases  described  in  the  Software  Requirements Specification (SRS) into specific test cases that imitate user interactions. These test cases include both positive and negative situations to ensure that the system performs as expected under various kinds of conditions. 

<table><tr><th colspan="1" rowspan="3" valign="top">![](eb73119a-d7c3-4901-8974-9decbd7603e1_00000.005.png)</th><th colspan="1" valign="top">ORGANISATION: </th><th colspan="1">PERFECT WAVE ACADEMY </th><th colspan="1" valign="top">SECTION: </th><th colspan="1" valign="top">SE </th></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">AREA BASED ON: </td><td colspan="1">SETIAWANGSA, KUALA LUMPUR </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">PAGE: </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">39/256            </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">DOCUMENT TITLE: </td><td colspan="3"><b>ACCEPTANCE TEST PLAN</b> FOR  Perfect Wave Academy (PWA): Swimming Class Management System </td></tr>
</table>

For example, a use case for booking a swimming session might include testing situations  in  which  the  session  is  available  and  successfully  booked,  as  well  as scenarios in which the booking fails due to a lack of available slots. The testing process establishes preconditions, such as the system's availability and the user's login, as well as  postconditions,  such  as  a  confirmation  message  being  delivered  following  a successful booking. By running these test cases, testers ensure that the system meets the requirements given in the use case. 

This testing technique provides realistic and practical validation, guaranteeing that the system satisfies user expectations and operates well in real-world scenarios. It also allows for the early discovery of process difficulties such as logical mistakes, errors in data, and faults in user interaction. Use Case-Based Testing guarantees that all system functionality is covered, making it an essential component of the Swimming Class Management System testing process. 

2. **Risk<a name="_page39_x69.00_y393.92"></a> Based Testing** 

A risk-based testing approach will be established to help prioritise and allocate testing resources. Due to time restrictions and limited resources, this strategy prioritises 

testing essential sections of the system first.  

This testing involves assessing the risk based on likelihood and impact of failures. These are the steps to conduct it include: 

- Analyze the requirements and its document.  
- Determine risk by calculating the likelihood and impact of each requirement through cost.  
- Prioritize risks through the total risk which is likelihood to occur multiply by impact.

<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">DOCUMENT TITLE: </td><td colspan="3"><b>ACCEPTANCE TEST PLAN</b> FOR  Perfect Wave Academy (PWA): Swimming Class Management System </td></tr>
</table>

***3.9.2.1. <a name="_page40_x145.00_y130.92"></a>Risk Analysis*** 

Risk-Based Testing prioritises functional testing based on the risk of failure and its effect.  This  technique  guarantees  that  essential  system  components,  such  as attendance  monitoring  and  payment  processing,  are  properly  tested  to  prevent catastrophic interruptions. Testing is focused towards locations with the greatest risk ratings, as established in the Risk Analysis Table. 

Formula (based on MSTB/ISTQB)  

- **Likelihood** from 1 (negligible) until 9 (very high)  
- **Impact** (non-linear scale of money values [e.g. 1,10,50,100,500,1000])  
- Total Risk = L \* I 



|Total Risk (r) |Priority Number |
| - | - |
|r < 3000 |1 (Lowest) |
|r >= 3000 |2 (Low) |
|r >= 4000 |3 (Medium) |
|r >= 8000 |4 (High) |
|r >= 9000 |5 (Highest) |

4. **TEST<a name="_page44_x69.00_y130.92"></a> CASES** 

Test cases contain a step-by-step flow of action where it will be performed by testers. Each test case contains tested by whom, test level, test type, test design technique, risk number, test  case  number,  test  case  name,  requirement  traceability,  test  case  description, preconditions, post conditions, item(s) to be tested, test procedure specification, input step(s), test data, expected result, actual result, pass/fail, and stakeholder (user).

<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Primary Actor </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">User </td></tr>

<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.1 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system prompts the user to choose a payment method. </td></tr>

<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.3 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system processes the payment using the selected method and contacts the authorization center for validation </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.4 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The authorization center validates the transaction. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.5 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top"><p>If the payment is successful: </p><p>- The system updates the payment status. </p><p>- The system triggers the "Notify User" use case to inform the user of successful payment. </p></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.6 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top"><p>If the payment fails: </p><p>￿  The system notifies the user of the failure and allows the user to retry. </p></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Post-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The payment method is selected, and the payment process is initiated. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="2" valign="top">Alternate Flow </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Pre-Condition  </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The user want to pay using different payment method</b> </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.1 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The user want to pay using eWallet. </td></tr>
</table>

<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Scenario </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Step </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Action </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="8" valign="top">Main Flow </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Pre-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The user must be registered in the system to log in as a student. If not registered, they can access limited features as a guest. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.1 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The user navigates to the login page. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.2  </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system displays a login form. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.3 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The user enters valid credentials (email and password). </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.4 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system verifies the credentials. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.5 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top"><p>If the credentials are valid: </p><p>￿  The system redirects the user to the main page. </p></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.6 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top"><p>If the credentials are invalid: </p><p>￿  The system displays an error message and allows the user to retry. </p></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Post-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The user is successfully logged into the system and redirected to the main page. If login fails, an error ![](eb73119a-d7c3-4901-8974-9decbd7603e1_00000.012.png)message is displayed. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="6" valign="top">Alternate Flow </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Pre-Condition  </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The want to login but forgot password</b> </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.1 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The user navigates to the login page. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.2 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system displays a login form. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.3 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The user enter their email on the textfield </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.4 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The user need to click “forgot password?” then automatic the renew password will send to the email </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.5 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Change the password by click the link that being </td></tr>
</table>

<table><tr><th colspan="1" valign="top">Use Case ID </th><th colspan="2" valign="top">UC_007 </th></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Use Case Name </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Renew Membership </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Description </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">This use case enables existing users to renew their membership plans when they expire or need extension. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Primary Actor </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">User  </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Include use cases </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Membership plan, Payment method </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Scenario </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Step </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Action </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="11" valign="top">Main Flow </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Pre-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The user must already have an account in the system with an expired or soon-to-expire ![](eb73119a-d7c3-4901-8974-9decbd7603e1_00000.011.png)membership. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.1 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The user logs into the system and navigates to the "Renew Membership" option on the main page. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.2  </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system prompts the user to choose a new membership plan. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.3 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The user selects a membership plan. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.4 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system verifies the credentials. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.5 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system prompts the user to select a payment method. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.6 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The user chooses from available payment methods (e.g., credit/debit card, PayPal, online banking, eWallet). </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.7 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system processes the payment through the selected payment method. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.8 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Upon successful payment, the system updates the user’s membership status. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.9 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system notifies the user that the renewal is successful. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Post-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The user’s membership is successfully renewed, and payment is completed. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Alternate Flow </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">- </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Robust Flow </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Pre-Condition  </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The user must already have an account in the system but might input invalid or incomplete details during renewal. </td></tr>
</table>

||2\.1 |User enters incorrect payment details. |

<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.2 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system logs attendance details (e.g., user ID, time, session). </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.3 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system saves the data successfully. </td></tr>

<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.2 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system notifies the user of the mismatch </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.3 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The user scans a new QR code or contacts the admin for further assistance. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Post-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The correct QR code is scanned, and attendance is logged successfully. </td></tr>
</table>

Attendance details (e.g., date, time, class) must be pre-registered and available in the ![](eb73119a-d7c3-4901-8974-9decbd7603e1_00000.015.png)system. </p></td></tr>

<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">AREA BASED ON: </td><td colspan="1">SETIAWANGSA, KUALA LUMPUR </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">PAGE: </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">67/256            </td></tr>

<table><tr><th colspan="1" valign="top">Use Case ID </th><th colspan="2" valign="top">UC_016 </th></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Use Case Name </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Update Cart </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Description </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Updates the user's shopping cart by adding, modifying, or removing items, and ensures the cart reflects the correct total and item details. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Primary Actor </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">System </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Include use cases </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">None </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Scenario </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Step </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Action </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="6" valign="top">Main Flow </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Pre-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top"><p>1. The user must have interacted with the cart (e.g., adding, modifying, or removing items). ![](eb73119a-d7c3-4901-8974-9decbd7603e1_00000.011.png)</p><p>2. Selected items must exist in the system inventory and be valid for purchase. </p></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.1 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system receives an action from the user (e.g., adding or modifying items via Add to Cart or Display Cart). </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.2 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system validates the item details (e.g., availability, pricing, discounts). </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.3 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system updates the cart with the changes (add, modify, or remove items). </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.4 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The cart summary, including updated totals, is displayed to the user. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Post-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top"><p>1. The cart is updated with the selected changes (e.g., added items, quantity changes, or ![](eb73119a-d7c3-4901-8974-9decbd7603e1_00000.020.png)removed items). </p><p>2. The updated cart details, including totals, are displayed to the user. </p></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Alternate Flow </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">- </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="4" valign="top">Robust Flow </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Pre-Condition  </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The user has added at least one item to the cart. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.1 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Leave the quantity field empty </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.2 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system displays an error message: "Quantity cannot be empty." </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Post-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The cart is not updated with invalid input. </td></tr>
</table>

<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">AREA BASED ON: </td><td colspan="1">SETIAWANGSA, KUALA LUMPUR </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">PAGE: </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">68/256            </td></tr>

<table><tr><th colspan="1" valign="top">Use Case ID </th><th colspan="2" valign="top">UC_017 </th></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Use Case Name </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Checkout </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Description </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Allows users to finalize their purchase and proceed with the payment process. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Primary Actor </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Guest, Student, Coach </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Include use cases </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Update Cart, Notify User </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Scenario </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Step </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Action </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="7" valign="top">Main Flow </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Pre-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top"><p>1. The user must have items in their cart </p><p>2. Selected items must be valid and in stock. </p></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.1 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The user reviews the cart and clicks "Checkout." </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.2 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system triggers Update Cart to ensure the cart is valid. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.3 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system prompts the user to select a payment method and complete the payment process. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.4 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system processes the payment and generates a receipt. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.5 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system triggers Notify User to send a purchase confirmation. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Post-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top"><p>1. The purchase process is initiated, and payment details are collected. </p><p>2. The user receives a notification confirming the purchase status. </p></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Alternate Flow </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">- </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="4" valign="top">Robust Flow </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Pre-Condition  </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system has access to a payment gateway for processing details. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.1 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Enter invalid payment details. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.2 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system displays an error: "Invalid payment details. Please check and try again."  </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Post-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The user is prompted to re-enter payment details, and the cart is not cleared. </td></tr>
</table>

<a name="_page68_x69.00_y717.92"></a>*Table 23 Use Case Statement - Shopping Management (Checkout)* 

<table><tr><th colspan="1" rowspan="3" valign="top">![](eb73119a-d7c3-4901-8974-9decbd7603e1_00000.005.png)</th><th colspan="1" valign="top">ORGANISATION: </th><th colspan="1">PERFECT WAVE ACADEMY </th><th colspan="1" valign="top">SECTION: </th><th colspan="1" valign="top">SE </th></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">AREA BASED ON: </td><td colspan="1">SETIAWANGSA, KUALA LUMPUR </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">PAGE: </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">69/256            </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">DOCUMENT TITLE: </td><td colspan="3"><b>ACCEPTANCE TEST PLAN</b> FOR  Perfect Wave Academy (PWA): Swimming Class Management System </td></tr>

<table><tr><th colspan="1" valign="top">Use Case ID </th><th colspan="2" valign="top">UC_018 </th></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Use Case Name </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Notify User </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Description </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Sends a notification to the user about their purchase or cart updates. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Primary Actor </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">System </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Include use cases </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">None  </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Scenario </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Step </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Action </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="6" valign="top">Main Flow </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Pre-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The user has performed an action requiring a notification (e.g., checkout, cart update). </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.1 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system detects an event requiring a notification (e.g., successful purchase, updated cart). </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.2 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system retrieves the user’s notification preferences (e.g., email, push notifications). </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.3 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system sends the notification to the user. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.4 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The user views the notification. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Post-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The user receives a notification about the action (e.g. purchase confirmation, cart changes). </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Alternate Flow </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">- </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Robust Flow </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">- </td></tr>
</table>

<table><tr><th colspan="1" valign="top">Use Case ID </th><th colspan="2" valign="top">UC_021 </th></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Use Case Name </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Approval Request </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Description </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">This use case describes the process by which a coach reviews and approves or rejects a session booking request from a student. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Primary Actor </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Coach </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Include use cases </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Accept, Reject </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Scenario </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Step </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Action </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="5" valign="top">Main Flow </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Pre-Condition </td><td colspan="1">The coach decides to approve the session booking request. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.1 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The coach selects the "Accept" option for the session request. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.2 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system updates the booking status to "Approved." </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.3 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system sends a notification to the student confirming the approval. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Post-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The booking request is approved, and the student is notified. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="6" valign="top">Alternate Flow (Reject Request) </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Pre-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The coach decides to reject the session booking request. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.1 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The coach selects the "Reject" option for the session request. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.2 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system prompts the coach to optionally enter a reason for rejection. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.3 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system updates the booking status to "Rejected." </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.4 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system sends a notification to the student informing them of the rejection. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Post-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The booking request is rejected, and the student is notified. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Robust Flow </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">- </td></tr>
</table>

<table><tr><th colspan="1" valign="top">Use Case ID </th><th colspan="3" valign="top">UC_024 </th></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Use Case Name </td><td colspan="3" valign="top">Update System </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Description </td><td colspan="3" valign="top">The system automatically updates session statuses, bookings for normal and one to one session, and notifications after key events, such as approval or deletion. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Primary Actor </td><td colspan="3" valign="top">System </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Include use cases </td><td colspan="3" valign="top">None </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Scenario </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Step </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Action </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="6" valign="top">Main Flow </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Pre-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">An event, such as session approval or deletion, must trigger the system update. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2" valign="top">1\.1 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">An event, such as session approval, occurs. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2" valign="top">1\.2 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system updates the session or booking status. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2" valign="top">1\.3 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Notifications are sent to all affected users. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2" valign="top">1\.4 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system ensures data consistency in the schedule and database </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2" valign="top">Post-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system reflects accurate session statuses and notifications. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Alternate Flow </td><td colspan="3" valign="top">- </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="7" valign="top">Robust Flow </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Pre-Condition </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">The student needs to cancel the session booked </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.1 </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">The student student want to cancel session </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.2 </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Student click the cancel the session  </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.3 </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">The system popup a message “Are you sure want to delete this session?” </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.4 </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">The user click yes </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.5 </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">The system not update the cancel session </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Post-Condition </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">The session status is not updated to "cancelled." </td></tr>
</table>

<table><tr><th colspan="1" valign="top">Use Case ID </th><th colspan="2" valign="top">UC_025 </th></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Use Case Name </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Notify User </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Description </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Automatically notifies students or coaches of updates related to their booking requests, such as approval, rejection, or changes to sessions. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Primary Actor </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">System </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Include use cases </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Approval Request, One-to-One Session, Normal Session </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Scenario </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Step </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Action </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="7" valign="top">Main Flow </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Pre-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">A booking request must be submitted, updated, or approved. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.1 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system identifies booking status updates (e.g., approval, rejection, session modification).  </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.2 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system retrieves the user information (email, app notification details).  </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.3 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system generates a notification with the relevant details.  </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.4 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The notification is sent via email or displayed in the application.  </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.5 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The user acknowledges the notification </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Post-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The user receives a notification about their booking status or session updates. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Alternate Flow </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">- </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Robust Flow </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">- </td></tr>
</table>

<table><tr><th colspan="1" valign="top">Use Case ID </th><th colspan="3" valign="top">UC_027 </th></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Use Case Name </td><td colspan="3" valign="top">Create Forum </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Description </td><td colspan="3" valign="top">This use case allows users to engage in a discussion forum to share experiences, ask questions, and exchange knowledge related to swimming. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Primary Actor </td><td colspan="3" valign="top">Student, Guest </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Include use cases </td><td colspan="3" valign="top">Reply Forum </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Scenario </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Step </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Action </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="7" valign="top">Main Flow </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Pre-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The user must be logged into the system or access as a guest. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2" valign="top">1\.1 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">User accesses the forum section. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2" valign="top">1\.2 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">User views the list of discussion threads. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2" valign="top">1\.3 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">User selects a thread to participate in. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2" valign="top">1\.4 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">User submits a new post or reply to an existing one. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2" valign="top">1\.5 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">System records the post/reply. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2" valign="top">Post-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The forum post or reply is successfully recorded in the system. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Alternate Flow </td><td colspan="3" valign="top">- </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="4" valign="top">Robust Flow </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Pre-Condition </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">The user has navigated to the forum section </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.1 </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">The user attempt to create a forum with an empty title and content. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.2 </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">The system displays an error message: "Title and content is required. Please provide a title and content for your forum post."  </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Post-Condition </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">The system does not create the forum post for invalid inputs. </td></tr>
</table>

<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Include use cases </td><td colspan="3" valign="top">None </td></tr>

<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.2 </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Error: "Reply cannot be empty." </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Post-Condition </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Reply is not stored for invalid inputs. </td></tr>
</table>

<table><tr><th colspan="1" valign="top">Use Case ID </th><th colspan="3" valign="top">UC_029 </th></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Use Case Name </td><td colspan="3" valign="top">Add Resources </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Description </td><td colspan="3" valign="top">Enables the admin to upload and add new learning resources (e.g., videos) to the system. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Primary Actor </td><td colspan="3" valign="top">Admin </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Include use cases </td><td colspan="3" valign="top">Manage Resources </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Scenario </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Step </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Action </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="7" valign="top">Main Flow </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Pre-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The admin must have access to the system and the necessary permissions. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2" valign="top">1\.1 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The admin logs into the system. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2" valign="top">1\.2 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system provides an option to upload new resources. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2" valign="top">1\.3 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The admin uploads the resource and provides relevant metadata (e.g., title, description). </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2" valign="top">1\.4 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system validates and stores the resource. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2" valign="top">1\.5 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The new resource is displayed in the self-learning module. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2" valign="top">Post-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The new resource is added to the system and becomes available to users. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Alternate Flow </td><td colspan="3" valign="top">- </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="4" valign="top">Robust Flow </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Pre-Condition </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">The resource upload form is accessible. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.1 </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">The admin provide empty title </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.2 </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Error: "Title cannot be empty." </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Post-Condition </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Invalid metadata (empty fields, long title, or description) is not accepted by the system.  </td></tr>
</table>

<table><tr><th colspan="1" valign="top">Use Case ID </th><th colspan="3" valign="top">UC_030 </th></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Use Case Name </td><td colspan="3" valign="top">Edit Resources </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Description </td><td colspan="3" valign="top">Enables the admin to update details of an existing resource, such as its title, description, or file. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Primary Actor </td><td colspan="3" valign="top">Admin </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Include use cases </td><td colspan="3" valign="top">None </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Scenario </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Step </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Action </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="6" valign="top">Main Flow </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Pre-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The resource must exist in the system. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2" valign="top">1\.1 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The admin selects a resource to edit. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2" valign="top">1\.2 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system displays the current details of the resource. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2" valign="top">1\.3 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The admin updates the details and submits the changes </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2" valign="top">1\.4 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The system validates and saves the updates </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="2" valign="top">Post-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">The resource is updated and available with the new details. </td></tr>

<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.3 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Admin provides the required details. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.4 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">System saves changes and updates the records. </td></tr>

<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Description </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Allows the system to generate attendance reports based on data.  </td></tr>

<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.1 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Admin/system triggers "Generate Reports." </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.2 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">System processes attendance data. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.3 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Reports are generated and saved. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Post-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Reports are generated and saved.  </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Alternate Flow </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">- </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" rowspan="5" valign="top">Robust Flow </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Pre-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">No attendance data exists for the selected date range. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.1 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Admin triggers "Generate Reports." </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.2 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">System identifies that no attendance data is available for the selected period. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2\.3 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">System displays an error message: "No attendance data available for the selected period." </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Post-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">No report is generated due to the absence of data for the selected period. </td></tr>

<table><tr><th colspan="1" valign="top">Use Case ID </th><th colspan="2" valign="top">UC_041 </th></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Use Case Name </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">View Reports  </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Description </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Allows the admin to view attendance reports. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Primary Actor </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Admin  </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Include use cases </td><td colspan="2" valign="top">Generate Reports </td></tr>

<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.1 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Admin navigates to "View Reports." </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1\.2 </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">System retrieves and displays reports. </td></tr>

<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">Post-Condition </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">Admin can view attendance reports.  </td></tr>

<table><tr><th colspan="3" valign="top"><b>Tested By</b> </th><th colspan="4" valign="top">Imran Hakimi Bin Abu Bakar </th><th colspan="1" valign="top"><b>Test Level</b> </th><th colspan="2" valign="top">User Acceptance Testing </th></tr>
<tr><td colspan="3" valign="top"><b>Test Type</b> </td><td colspan="7" valign="top">Black Box Testing – Functional Testing </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="3" valign="top"><b>Test Design Technique</b> </td><td colspan="7" valign="top">Use Case Testing </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="3" valign="top"><b>Risk Number</b> </td><td colspan="7" valign="top">3 (Medium) </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="3" valign="top"><b>Test Case Number</b> </td><td colspan="7" valign="top">TC024.3 </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="3" valign="top"><b>Test Case Name</b> </td><td colspan="7" valign="top">Lost Internet Connection During Session Status Update </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="3" valign="top"><b>Requirement Traceability</b> </td><td colspan="7" valign="top">REQ_F2401 </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="3" valign="top"><b>Test Case Description</b>  </td><td colspan="7" valign="top">Verifying that the system gracefully handles a failure (lost internet connection) during session status updates. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="10" valign="top"><b>Item(s) to be tested</b> </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1 </td><td colspan="9" valign="top">The system’s ability to recover from a lost internet connection during session status update. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2 </td><td colspan="9" valign="top">The system’s ability to retry the session and status update. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">3 </td><td colspan="9" valign="top">The notification mechanism’s ability to retry or handle failure </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="4" rowspan="4" valign="top"><b>Pre-condition</b> </td><td colspan="6" valign="top">1\.  The session exists and is available for booking. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="6" valign="top">2\.  The student is logged into the system. </td></tr>

<tr><td colspan="2" valign="top"><b>Test Case Description</b>  </td><td colspan="6">Verifying that the system handles a failure due to a lost internet connection while retrieving statistics data and ensures recovery. </td></tr>

<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">1 </td><td colspan="7" valign="top">The system’s ability to handle a lost internet connection while retrieving statistics data. </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">2 </td><td colspan="7" valign="top">The system’s ability to retrieve the data again once the connection is restored. </td></tr>

<tr><td colspan="5">2\.  Simulate a lost internet connection during the process of retrieving statistics.</td></tr>

<tr><td colspan="3" valign="top">None  </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">None  </td><td colspan="1">Once the connection is restored, the system retries retrieving the data. </td><td colspan="2"></td><td colspan="1"></td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="3" valign="top"><b>Post-condition</b> </td><td colspan="5">The system retrieves and displays the statistics once the internet connection is restored and the failure is resolved. </td></tr>

5. **TEST<a name="_page243_x69.00_y130.92"></a> ENVIRONMENT** 

This chapter section outlines the required resources that are important and part of the test environment in order to execute the testing. These resources are the hardware, software, facilities, distinctive test tools and other necessary special requirement necessary. 

1. **Test<a name="_page243_x69.00_y229.92"></a> Site** 

<a name="_page243_x69.00_y260.92"></a>**5.1.1 Developer Site** 

Testing will take place in test lab environment where testers execute few testing from the regular workstations. 

2. **Facilities<a name="_page243_x69.00_y344.92"></a> required** 

To facilitate effective testing in the dedicated lab environment, specific facilities are essential: 

1. **Lab<a name="_page243_x69.00_y432.92"></a> Space** 

A testing lab with an approximate area of 500 square feet will be designated for testing purposes. Within this space, there will be allocated desktop areas covering 200 square feet to accommodate workstations for testers. 

2. **Furniture<a name="_page243_x69.00_y539.92"></a>** 

The lab will be equipped with tables and chairs to provide workstations for the testing team. The arrangement of furniture will be designed to facilitate effective communication and collaboration among team members. Tables and chairs will be positioned close to each other, facing each other, to promote seamless communication and convenient team meetings. 

3. **Power<a name="_page243_x69.00_y683.92"></a> Infrastructure** 

Multiple plug points will be strategically placed near workstations to ensure uninterrupted access to power for testing devices and equipment.  

<table><tr><th colspan="1" rowspan="3" valign="top">![](eb73119a-d7c3-4901-8974-9decbd7603e1_00000.005.png)</th><th colspan="1" valign="top">ORGANISATION: </th><th colspan="1">PERFECT WAVE ACADEMY </th><th colspan="1" valign="top">SECTION: </th><th colspan="1" valign="top">SE </th></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">AREA BASED ON: </td><td colspan="1">SETIAWANGSA, KUALA LUMPUR </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">PAGE: </td><td colspan="1" valign="top">244/256          </td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="1" valign="top">DOCUMENT TITLE: </td><td colspan="3"><b>ACCEPTANCE TEST PLAN</b> FOR  Perfect Wave Academy (PWA): Swimming Class Management System </td></tr>
</table>

4. **Communication<a name="_page244_x69.00_y130.92"></a> Facilities** 

To enhance collaboration and communication among the testing team, the lab layout will be designed to encourage face-to-face interaction. This setup aims to facilitate efficient discussions and meetings during the testing process. 

5. **Network<a name="_page244_x69.00_y229.92"></a> Connectivity** 

A stable and secure network connection will be established within the testing lab to ensure seamless communication, access to external resources, and the ability to perform online testing activities.  

This comprehensive testing environment is designed to provide the necessary infrastructure and resources for thorough testing of the Perfect Wave Academy app,  ensuring  that  all  aspects  of  functionality,  performance,  and  user experience are rigorously evaluated in a controlled and conducive setting.

7. **DEFINTIONS,<a name="_page254_x69.00_y130.92"></a> ACRONYMS AND ABBREVIATION** 
1. **Definitions<a name="_page254_x69.00_y153.92"></a>** 



|**Term** |**Definition** |
| - | - |
|Coding |Generation of source code. |
|Defect |Non-conformance to requirements or functional / program specification. |
|Functional Testing |Testing the features and operational behaviour of a product to ensure they correspond to its specifications |
|Risk Analysis |The process of assessing identified risks to estimate their impact and probability of occurrence (likelihood). |
|Software Requirements Specification |A deliverable that describes all data, functional and behavioural requirements, all constraints, and all validation requirements for software. |
|Test Item |The individual element to be tested. There usually is one test object and many test items. See also test object |
|Tester |A technically skilled professional who is involved in testing of a component or system |
